{"forms":[{"form":"canonical forms","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"A canonical form is an element of a set of representatives of equivalence classes of forms such that there is a function or procedure which projects every element of each equivalence class onto that one element, the canonical form of that equivalence class. The canonical form is expected to be simpler than the rest of the forms in some way. For example, for cubic polynomials acted upon by the group of translations along the abscissa, the canonical forms are cubic polynomials without a quadratic term.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A standard or normal presentation of a mathematical entity or a text string, etc."],"tags":["countable","uncountable"],"topics":["computing","engineering","mathematics","natural-sciences","physical-sciences","sciences"]},{"glosses":["Any of a set of representations of the resonance structure of a molecule each of which contributes to the real structure; a contributing structure."],"tags":["countable","physical","uncountable"],"topics":["chemistry","natural-sciences","physical-sciences"]},{"glosses":["Synonym of dictionary form."],"synonyms":[{"tags":["synonym","synonym-of"],"word":"dictionary form"}],"tags":["countable","rare","uncountable"],"topics":["human-sciences","linguistics","sciences"]}],"synonyms":[{"topics":["mathematics","sciences"],"word":"normal form"},{"topics":["mathematics","sciences"],"word":"standard form"},{"topics":["linguistics","human-sciences","sciences"],"word":"citation form"},{"topics":["linguistics","human-sciences","sciences"],"word":"dictionary form"},{"topics":["linguistics","human-sciences","sciences"],"word":"basic form"}],"upstream_line":1149598,"upstream_offset":4100620996,"upstream_record_sha256":"f7617a1741a77f4cdbdb5bdd1c592088db603502b4e2c9dd4413a8f486d75ab8","word":"canonical form"}

He was so nice to you.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To discard or abandon; to stop associating with (someone)."],"synonyms":[{"word":"abandon"},{"word":"desert"},{"word":"discard"},{"word":"dump"},{"word":"jettison"},{"word":"leave"},{"word":"lose"},{"word":"forsake"},{"word":"quit"},{"word":"shed"},{"word":"strand"}],"tags":["transitive"]},{"antonyms":[{"word":"stand by"},{"word":"stick by"}],"examples":[{"text":"When the second engine failed, the pilot was forced to ditch; their last location was just south of the Azores.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To discard or abandon; to stop associating with (someone).","To deliberately crash-land an airplane on water."],"synonyms":[{"word":"abandon"},{"word":"desert"},{"word":"discard"},{"word":"dump"},{"word":"jettison"},{"word":"leave"},{"word":"lose"},{"word":"forsake"},{"word":"quit"},{"word":"shed"},{"word":"strand"}],"tags":["ambitransitive","transitive"],"topics":["aeronautics","aerospace","aviation","business","engineering","natural-sciences","physical-sciences"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The truant officer caught Louise ditching with her friends, and her parents were forced to pay a fine.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To deliberately not attend classes; to play hookey."],"tags":["ambitransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Enclosure led to fuller winter employment in hedging and ditching.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To dig ditches."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The soldiers ditched the tent to prevent flooding.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To dig ditches.","To dig ditches around."],"tags":["intransitive","transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The engine was ditched and turned on its side.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To throw into a ditch."],"tags":["transitive"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/dɪt͡ʃ/"}],"upstream_line":175356,"upstream_offset":829849507,"upstream_record_sha256":"767df71df5ecff2b74eb2ffbaa1a481227f9a73afdac187e2c401910a5587623","word":"ditch"}
